# Parity matrix

This file is the contract. Each row is one guarantee the original, [ai-job-search](https://github.com/MadsLorentzen/ai-job-search)
(v1.7.2 plus 65 commits), makes, derived from its command specs, skills, tools and tests (not from memory), with the place
it lives here and the test that pins it. **Every commit that adds behaviour updates it. Done means no row says `planned`.**

Status is one of `done`, `planned`, `stricter here` (the rebuild enforces in code what the original only said in prose), or
`not ported, because ...`. Rows still marked `planned (stricter: ...)` are planned *and* will be stricter than the original.

The rows were written by seven readers, each reading a slice of the original completely, and were not edited for wording.
Rows from different slices overlap in places; overlaps are merged as the module is built.

**Slices**

- [A. Profile, privacy, security guards](#a-profile-privacy-security-guards): 225 rows
- [B. Search state, dedupe, ranking, fit](#b-search-state-dedupe-ranking-fit): 274 rows
- [C1. The apply workflow and its checklist](#c1-the-apply-workflow-and-its-checklist): 272 rows
- [C2. PDF and layout verification, salary benchmark](#c2-pdf-and-layout-verification-salary-benchmark): 163 rows
- [D. Application lifecycle, interview, upskill, report](#d-application-lifecycle-interview-upskill-report): 217 rows
- [E1. Boards (LinkedIn, freehire, Jobbank) and add-portal](#e1-boards-linkedin-freehire-jobbank-and-add-portal): 189 rows
- [E2. Danish boards (Jobindex, Jobnet, Jobdanmark)](#e2-danish-boards-jobindex-jobnet-jobdanmark): 149 rows

Total: 1489 rows.

## A. Profile, privacy, security guards

# Parity slice A: profile, onboarding, privacy, security guards, robots, web research

1. Files read in full: CLAUDE.md, README.md, SETUP.md, documents/README.md, .claude/commands/setup.md, SKILL.md + 01/02/03/09 skill files, tools/security_guards.py, robots_check.py, lint_skills.py, and tests test_setup_command, test_onboarding_privacy, test_security_guards, test_robots_check, test_placeholder_integrity, test_tools_utf8_output, test_lint_skills.
2. Path legend: `setup.md` = .claude/commands/setup.md; `SK/` = .claude/skills/job-application-assistant/; `robots_check.py`, `security_guards.py`, `lint_skills.py` live under tools/; `tests/` as named.
3. Matrix limits: the original's onboarding is a prose spec with only substring tests, so most profile rows are "prose only, no test"; the rebuild's code-checked grounding, eligibility/hours/start-date, source-tagged GitHub enrichment and "person confirms before writing" have no original text (see the last section, rows cite nearest original lines). `/expand`'s spec is outside this slice; only README:143 describes it.
4. Surprises: (a) the Languages table and Language Gate are only prose, zero tests; (b) the "confirm before writing" rule is prose in setup.md with no test, while the *privacy* preflight is order-tested; (c) security_guards' REQUIRED_IGNORE_RULES checks rule presence by exact-line set membership, and a separate test shows pattern behaviour only for upskill and interview paths; (d) robots_check docstring says "go to escalation step 3" but 09-web-research says step 4 (stale numbering); (e) test_tools_utf8_output pins UTF-8 stdout for tools outside this slice (rank_state, job_key, verify_*), only the contract carries over.
5. Row count: 211 rows across 14 topic tables (5 out-of-scope, 3 owner-addition rows with no original text).

### Privacy preflight and personal-data protection

| Original guarantee (file:line) | Where it lives here | How it's checked (test name) | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| Before anything is written, the remote origin is inspected (not after, since the closing note fires when files already exist) (setup.md:13-19, tests/test_onboarding_privacy.py:210-229) | guards.publish_preflight | test_origin_checked_before_any_profile_write | planned (stricter: the writer refuses to run until the preflight result is recorded) |
| A missing remote, a failing remote lookup, or a non-git directory skips the check silently (setup.md:15-17) | guards.publish_preflight | test_no_origin_or_not_a_checkout_skips_silently | planned |
| A public fork, or a remote whose visibility cannot be determined, triggers a warning and a wait for the person's confirmation before the path prompt; private/no origin/non-fork needs none (setup.md:17-30) | guards.publish_preflight | test_public_or_unknown_visibility_fork_warns_and_waits | planned (stricter: unknown visibility is treated as public by code) |
| The warning names the data at risk (name, contact, employment history, salary), says pushing to a fork exposes it, and offers two safe options: keep commits local or use a private repository (setup.md:21-27) | guards.publish_preflight.warning_text | test_warning_mentions_data_and_two_safe_options | planned |
| The closing summary carries a privacy note that the generated files are tracked and a fork is always public so commits must not be pushed there (setup.md:416-419) | profile.onboard.finalize | test_closing_summary_has_privacy_note | planned |
| README and SETUP warn about public forks immediately next to the fork command, mention personal data in tracked files, and point at the private-remote recipe (tests/test_onboarding_privacy.py:199-207, README.md:81-88, SETUP.md:184-190) | docs / guards.publish_preflight | test_docs_warn_next_to_fork_command | planned (docs content check) |
| The fork command is paired with setting the default repo so issues/PRs never silently land on the public upstream tracker (SETUP.md:166-182) | guards.publish_preflight | test_no_default_remote_pointing_at_public_upstream | planned |
| Sensitive artefacts (tracker, salary data, documents subfolders, application archives, reports, company research cache, env files, local Claude settings, ATS text extractions, tailored CV/letter files) are gitignored and never enter git (security_guards.py:66-116, SETUP.md:310) | guards.gitignore_rules | test_required_ignore_rules_all_present | planned |
| Each required ignore rule, when missing, is reported by name with an actionable message (security_guards.py:231-238, tests/test_security_guards.py:228-236) | guards.gitignore_rules | test_each_missing_ignore_rule_reported | planned |
| Extra ignore rules are allowed (tests/test_security_guards.py:239-242) | guards.gitignore_rules | test_extra_ignore_rules_do_not_fail | planned |
| Generated report ignore rules (reports, upskill markdown, depth-independent upskill report prefix) are all required (tests/test_security_guards.py:244-260) | guards.gitignore_rules | test_report_ignore_rules_required | planned |
| Any negation line (`!pattern`) outside a small reviewed allowlist fails, because a later negation can silently re-expose a path whose ignore rule is still present (security_guards.py:118-130, security_guards.py:239-247, tests/test_security_guards.py:338-358) | guards.gitignore_rules | test_unlisted_negation_fails_allowlisted_passes | planned |
| Ignore patterns are tested by behaviour, not only presence: upskill reports ignored at any depth, the skill's own SKILL.md stays tracked (tests/test_security_guards.py:279-302) | guards.gitignore_rules | test_upskill_report_ignored_at_depth_skill_md_tracked | planned (stricter: uses a real git check-ignore over every path the loop writes) |
| The path the interview command writes its prep pack to is derived from the command spec and must be ignored by the shipped rules, so moving the output fails the test (tests/test_security_guards.py:304-335) | guards.gitignore_rules | test_every_loop_output_path_is_gitignored | planned (stricter: derived from the loop's real output paths) |
| Depth-independent ignore rules exist for scraper state files because skills resolve their data directory relative to themselves (security_guards.py:69-74, 98-104) | guards.gitignore_rules | test_state_files_ignored_at_any_depth | planned |
| Template placeholders must survive in the pristine template and be destroyed by personalisation, so a CI check on committed personal data actually fires (tests/test_placeholder_integrity.py:1-30, 266-317) | profile.placeholders / guards | test_personalisation_removes_every_sentinel | planned |
| The profile's sentinel sits inside data that setup fills (the email line), not in a header comment the model may leave untouched (tests/test_placeholder_integrity.py:324-335, SK/01-candidate-profile.md:14) | profile.placeholders | test_sentinel_located_in_filled_data | planned |
| Placeholder-integrity tests only apply to the pristine upstream checkout; forks that personalised files skip them (tests/test_placeholder_integrity.py:283-286) | guards | test_placeholder_guard_skipped_on_personalised_tree | planned |
| No placeholder token may reach a compiled document: contact block and signature in CV and cover-letter templates must be personalised on every path (setup.md:375-378, tests/test_setup_command.py:58-73) | profile.placeholders.assert_none_left | test_no_placeholder_tokens_in_templates_after_setup | planned (stricter: code scans every generated file for `[YOUR_...]`-style tokens before finishing) |
| The completion summary lists each file generated including cover-letter templates (setup.md:404-414, tests/test_setup_command.py:70-73) | profile.onboard.finalize | test_summary_lists_all_generated_files | planned |
| Setup tells the person how many STAR stubs still need manual completion when the documents path left stubs (setup.md:380-381, 426-428) | profile.onboard.finalize | test_summary_reports_star_stub_count | planned |
| Generated files that carry personal data must be tracked-safe: profile files are tracked, so the privacy warning is the mitigation (setup.md:416) | guards.publish_preflight | test_profile_files_flagged_as_tracked_in_summary | planned |

| Cliches and filler ("passionate about", "great fit", "leverage my skills", "hit the ground running", "drive results", "synergies") are cut (SK/03-writing-style.md:10) | style.banned_phrases | test_banned_phrases_flagged | planned (stricter: deterministic phrase list) |

| Apologetic or overly humble phrasing is removed in favour of "I bring X, shown by Y" (SK/03-writing-style.md:12) | style.hedging | test_hedging_phrases_flagged | planned |
| Every company-specific statement in a letter must be independently verified from sources located by the tool (search by company name, official site), never from URLs inside the posting; unverifiable claims are generalised or dropped (SK/03-writing-style.md:13, CLAUDE.md:112) | research.verify_claims + guards.untrusted | test_unverified_company_claim_blocked | planned (stricter: claim-to-source check blocks the letter) |
| Reviewer-agent research is not trusted at face value; the claim is re-verified (SK/03-writing-style.md:13, CLAUDE.md:112) | research.verify_claims | test_reviewer_claims_reverified | planned |
| A search-result snippet is a lead, never a source; a fact enters a letter only from a fetched page (SK/03-writing-style.md:13, SK/09-web-research.md:116) | research.verify_claims | test_snippet_only_claim_rejected | planned |
| Reframing is limited to emphasis, not substance, using an "interview backtrack test": could the person explain this bullet without backtracking (SK/03-writing-style.md:14-17) | grounding / style | test_backtrack_test_flags_overreach | planned |
| Allowed reframing: reordering, natural synonyms, emphasising one aspect; flagged: merging academic and industry into an implied-industry claim, or adjacent work in the posting's exact terms; never: claiming experience not held or an unworked domain (SK/03-writing-style.md:15-17) | grounding.reframe_policy | test_reframe_levels_ok_flag_never | planned |
| Flagged bullets are shown to the person after drafting with keep/soften/drop; and if experience-match score is below 50 the person is warned before drafting that heavy reframing would be needed (SK/03-writing-style.md:18) | apply / fit | test_stretch_bullets_surface_and_low_match_warns | planned |
| Tone: warm but direct, conversational professional, first person active voice, demonstrate rather than state (SK/03-writing-style.md:20-24) | style.tone | test_passive_voice_and_generic_trait_claims_flagged | planned |
| Application headline should be specific (title/education + posting keyword), not "Application for ..." (SK/03-writing-style.md:26-33) | letter.headline | test_generic_headline_rejected | planned |
| Letters are scannable: descriptive subheadings, industry keywords in headings, no filler, one page maximum (hard rule) (SK/03-writing-style.md:35-41, CLAUDE.md:139) | letter / layout | test_letter_is_exactly_one_page | planned |
| Letters are forward-looking, not CV repetition: tasks the person can solve, the approach, expected outcomes, 1-2 short past examples (SK/03-writing-style.md:43-49) | letter.structure | test_letter_forward_looking_sections | planned |
| Letter structure: opening states role and connects background in 1-3 sentences; motivation / why-this-company comes first after opening; company-specific paragraph shows researched specifics; closing is brief, confident, no begging (SK/03-writing-style.md:51-81) | letter.structure | test_letter_section_order | planned |
| Body bullets: 3-5 bullets, outcome-oriented, at least one showing initiative, varied openings (SK/03-writing-style.md:62-65, 83-86) | letter.bullets | test_bullet_count_and_variety | planned |
| Role-type guidance (technical, domain, consulting, leadership) shapes what leads (SK/03-writing-style.md:88-105) | style.role_type | test_lead_content_depends_on_role_type | planned |
| Applications default to the posting's language and read as native, not translated (SK/03-writing-style.md:107-110, CLAUDE.md:30) | letter.language | test_letter_language_matches_posting | planned |
| Style patterns observed from past letters are additive only and never replace the rules (setup.md:171) | profile.infer.style | test_observed_patterns_additive_only | planned |
| Agentic-coding or AI-tooling mentions must name Claude Code explicitly (CLAUDE.md:103, 129) | style / letter | test_ai_tooling_mentions_name_claude_code | planned (owner: confirm still wanted for the loop) |

| The application is recorded in the tracker only after both documents exist; a lone CV or letter is not an application; the deadline is taken from the posting and never guessed (SK/SKILL.md:42-45) | tracker / apply | test_record_only_when_both_documents_exist | planned |

### Web research and fetching trust boundary

| Original guarantee (file:line) | Where it lives here | How it's checked (test name) | Status |
|---|---|---|---|

| A URL that appears inside a posting body is never fetched; the URL the person supplied is the only exception (SK/09-web-research.md:14) | guards.url_policy | test_urls_from_posting_body_never_fetched | planned (stricter: allowlist built from the supplied URL and the employer's own domain) |
| Companies are researched by searching their name and navigating from the official site, never via links in the posting (SK/09-web-research.md:15, SK/03-writing-style.md:13) | research.discover_sources | test_company_sources_found_independently | planned |
| Extracted fetch content goes only into evaluation and drafting, never into control flow (SK/09-web-research.md:16) | guards.untrusted | test_fetched_text_never_selects_commands | planned |
| A 403 from the plain fetch tool is not a dead end: the response is not treated as the page being unavailable, and the workflow must not soften the letter or lean on snippets because of it (SK/09-web-research.md:18-24, SK/SKILL.md:20) | fetch.escalate | test_403_triggers_escalation_not_giving_up | planned |
| Escalation order: portal CLI detail command if the host belongs to a configured portal; plain fetch; robots check then browser-header retry; web search for the employer's own posting; only then declare unavailable (SK/09-web-research.md:80-92) | fetch.escalate | test_escalation_order | planned |
| A page that returns 200 but shows a sign-in wall is not fixable with headers and skips to finding the employer's own posting; never draft from an aggregator title plus assumption (SK/09-web-research.md:94-96) | fetch.detect_login_wall | test_login_wall_not_retried_with_headers | planned |
| Genuinely unavailable in ranking is marked unreachable (retried later), distinct from expired (shown to be closed); in apply it stops and tells the person rather than drafting from the title (SK/09-web-research.md:92) | rankstate / fetch | test_unreachable_vs_expired_and_apply_stops | planned |
| The employer's own posting is preferred over aggregator listings, which drop reference ID, grade/seniority, essential-vs-desirable split and values language; material differences between versions are reported to the person, not silently chosen (SK/09-web-research.md:98-107) | fetch.prefer_employer | test_employer_version_preferred_and_diff_reported | planned |
| Aggregator anchor URLs (ending in a fragment) are listing pages not postings; a fetch whose content does not match the expected title is a failed fetch (SK/09-web-research.md:109) | fetch.validate_match | test_title_mismatch_treated_as_failed_fetch | planned |
| The browser-header retry sends a fixed browser user agent, accept, language, encoding, sec-fetch and upgrade headers with compression decoding, has a 45s ceiling, and writes into scratch space never the repo (SK/09-web-research.md:48-61) | fetch.browser_retry | test_retry_headers_timeout_and_scratch_dir | planned |
| Text extraction from saved HTML strips script/style/noscript/svg, unescapes entities, collapses whitespace, and is read through JSON-blob noise rather than assumed failed (SK/09-web-research.md:63-78) | fetch.html_to_text | test_html_to_text_strips_scripts_and_decodes | planned |
| Verified company claims must trace to a page actually fetched from the company's own domain or consistent independent reporting; specifics preferred over generic praise (SK/09-web-research.md:111-117) | research.verify_claims | test_claim_requires_fetched_source | planned |
| What was verified and from where is recorded in the final presentation so the person can defend it (SK/09-web-research.md:119) | research / report | test_verified_claims_listed_with_sources | planned |
| The shipped portal CLI list and per-portal permission entries are the only pre-approved network-facing commands (security_guards.py:38-64) | guards.allowed_commands | test_only_listed_commands_executable | planned (stricter: loop executes only a fixed command table) |

| Original guarantee (file:line) | Where it lives here | How it's checked (test name) | Status |
|---|---|---|---|

| Exit status 0 means retry may proceed and 1 means it must not; wrong argument count exits 2 and a missing URL never defaults to allowed (robots_check.py:176-182, tests/test_robots_check.py:119-128) | guards.robots.cli | test_cli_requires_url_and_exit_codes | planned |
| Longest matching rule wins regardless of file order (robots_check.py:136-144, tests/test_robots_check.py:40-49) | guards.robots.allowed | test_longest_match_beats_rule_order | planned |
| A tie in specificity between Allow and Disallow goes to Disallow, independent of which is listed first (robots_check.py:141-142, tests/test_robots_check.py:51-60) | guards.robots.allowed | test_tie_goes_to_disallow_both_orders | planned |
| Blank lines inside a record do not end it; rules after blank lines still apply (robots_check.py:93-117, tests/test_robots_check.py:33-38) | guards.robots.parse_groups | test_blank_lines_within_record_keep_rules | planned |
| The standard-library robots parser must not be used because it fails open on blank lines and file-order matching (SK/09-web-research.md:46) | guards.robots | test_no_stdlib_robotparser_import | planned |
| Wildcard `*` in patterns matches any run, trailing `$` anchors the end, a mid-pattern `$` is literal (robots_check.py:119-134, tests/test_robots_check.py:66-74) | guards.robots.match | test_wildcard_end_anchor_and_literal_dollar | planned |
| Rule patterns are percent-decoded before matching the decoded path, so encoded rules do not silently skip; the encoded rule does not over-match (robots_check.py:119-134, tests/test_robots_check.py:76-87, 214-229) | guards.robots.match | test_percent_encoded_rules_match_decoded_path | planned |
| The request path plus query string is matched (query included, decoded) (robots_check.py:147-150, tests/test_robots_check.py:76-87) | guards.robots.gate | test_query_string_is_part_of_matched_path | planned |
| An empty Disallow value means allow everything; empty or comment-only file allows (robots_check.py:127-128, tests/test_robots_check.py:89-94) | guards.robots.allowed | test_empty_disallow_and_ruleless_allow | planned |
| Comments after `#` are stripped before parsing (robots_check.py:101, tests/test_robots_check.py:96-97) | guards.robots.parse_groups | test_inline_comments_stripped | planned |
| Agent names match case-insensitively; with no group for the named agent the `*` group applies; several User-agent lines share one rule set (robots_check.py:104-117, 138, tests/test_robots_check.py:100-116) | guards.robots.parse_groups, allowed | test_agent_selection_rules | planned |
| A named-agent opt-out group is honoured even when `*` allows (tests/test_robots_check.py:101-104) | guards.robots.allowed | test_named_agent_optout_honoured | planned |
| One leading byte-order mark is skipped everywhere (grouping, body recognition, undecodable check) so a BOM does not hide the first group and flip Disallow to allow; BOM-only file is empty and allows (robots_check.py:102, 55, 75, tests/test_robots_check.py:232-265) | guards.robots | test_bom_does_not_hide_first_group | planned |
| HTTP 404 on robots.txt means no published policy and is permission (robots_check.py:158-159, SK/09-web-research.md:41) | guards.robots.gate | test_404_means_allowed | planned |
| Any other failure to read the policy (non-200/404 status, network error, timeout, curl error) leaves permission unconfirmed and the retry does not happen (robots_check.py:169-170, SK/09-web-research.md:41) | guards.robots.gate | test_unreadable_policy_is_unconfirmed_not_allowed | planned |
| A 200 whose body is not a robots file (HTML or JSON error page) is treated as unreadable, not as allow-all; an empty or whitespace-only body is genuine allow-all; a sitemap-only file counts as a policy (robots_check.py:53-71, tests/test_robots_check.py:131-212) | guards.robots.is_robots_body | test_soft_200_unconfirmed_empty_allows_sitemap_counts | planned |
| A User-agent, Allow or Disallow line containing the replacement character (undecodable bytes) makes the policy unreadable, including a stray byte before the field name or after `*`; such a character in comments or other fields is harmless (robots_check.py:73-91, tests/test_robots_check.py:268-357) | guards.robots.has_undecodable_rule | test_undecodable_rule_line_unconfirmed_comment_harmless | planned |
| Valid non-ASCII (UTF-8) rules are still parsed and obeyed (tests/test_robots_check.py:348-357) | guards.robots | test_utf8_non_ascii_rules_obeyed | planned |
| Policy is fetched first with the honest agent, then as a browser if the honest request is refused, and the browser-fetched policy is then obeyed strictly (WAFs often block the policy file itself) (robots_check.py:153-167, SK/09-web-research.md:45, tests/test_robots_check.py:158-199) | guards.robots.gate | test_browser_fallback_for_policy_read_then_strict | planned |
| The robots URL is rebuilt from scheme and host, never passed through from the caller (robots_check.py:148-151, tests/test_robots_check.py:388-405) | guards.robots.gate | test_robots_target_rebuilt_from_scheme_host | planned |
| The fetch command ends option parsing before the URL so a dash-leading value cannot become an option, and a dash-leading CLI argument fails closed (robots_check.py:36-42, tests/test_robots_check.py:361-386) | guards.robots.fetch | test_dash_url_cannot_become_flag_fails_closed | planned |
| The policy fetch follows at most 5 redirects, has a 12s limit with a 20s process ceiling, and uses a real HTTP client subprocess rather than the stdlib client because some hosts hang the latter (robots_check.py:33-42) | guards.robots.fetch | test_fetch_limits_redirects_and_timeouts | planned |
| Policy bytes are decoded as UTF-8 with replacement regardless of host locale, so an undefined legacy-codepage byte never turns a readable policy into UNCONFIRMED; a 404 HTML page with such bytes still means no policy (robots_check.py:43-46, tests/test_robots_check.py:408-459) | guards.robots.fetch | test_utf8_decode_independent_of_locale | planned |

## B. Search state, dedupe, ranking, fit

# Parity slice B: scrape, search state, dedupe, ranking, fit rubric (+ /expand, portal CLI install)

1. Files read completely: expand.md, rank.md, job-scraper/SKILL.md + search-queries.md, 04-job-evaluation.md, tools/job_key.py, rank_state.py, install_portal_clis.py, and the 8 named test files (all under /tmp/claude-0/-home-user-job-search-loop/33daec6a-6b37-5293-a76c-f19ff1201740/scratchpad/src/ai-job-search). Paths in rows are relative to that checkout.
2. Rows: see the count in my reply. rank.md, SKILL.md, search-queries.md and 04 are cited by file line; tests are cited as `tests/<file>:line` where a test pins more than the spec says.
3. Matrix cannot express: the fetch-escalation ladder itself (it lives in 09-web-research.md, outside my slice; I only cite how rank.md and SKILL.md invoke it, and "robots check fails closed" is NOT stated in my files, only "if robots.txt permits"); per-portal CLI flags and field details (`.agents/skills/*`) beyond the contract sentence.
4. Surprises: (a) the 30-day staleness flag, mass-posting consolidation, bounded health check, LinkedIn `isActive:false` expiry and 14-day recency window are PROSE ONLY (no tool; tests just grep the spec text); (b) 04 says a language/eligibility FAIL means "do not score", but rank.md still scores and vetoes at output, and rank.md/rank_state have no eligibility-gate field at all; (c) `rank_state.apply` never validates verdict values (only the literal "FAIL" vetoes, so a typo ranks the job), and `urgent` is computed only on the scored path; (d) many tests pin spec wording by substring (e.g. "costs no fetch"), protection a Python rebuild replaces with behaviour tests; (e) search-queries.md Date Filter says keep if date unknown or deadline not passed, while SKILL.md 1b.3 says drop results older than 14 days client-side.
5. Out of scope in this slice: none by name (notion-sync and upskill appear only as extra paths in test_scrape_seen_jobs_path).

| Company slug is capped at 40 chars and title slug at 60, truncation followed by a 6-char hash of the FULL slug so the capped key is stable across runs and two long titles never collide (job_key.py:55-57, 98-109; tests/test_job_key.py:43-53) | jobkey.cap | test_long_titles_hash_capped_and_distinct | planned |
| Key shape is "<company-slug>_<title-slug>" (job_key.py:153; tests/test_job_key.py:38-41) | jobkey.make_key | test_key_shape | planned |
| A title that slugifies to nothing falls back to the portal's numeric job id (first run of 6+ digits in the URL) so a non-Latin posting never yields a bare "company_" key (job_key.py:63, 125-134; tests/test_job_key.py:55-61) | jobkey.make_key | test_non_latin_title_uses_url_job_id | planned |
| With no 6-digit run in the URL the fallback is a hash of the URL alone (not the title), so a re-listed posting with a changed or recased title keeps one key; with no URL the title is hashed; an empty title with no URL gets "untitled-<hash>" (job_key.py:136-153; tests/test_job_key.py:63-65, 73-91, 145-156) | jobkey.make_key | test_url_hash_fallback_stable_across_title_changes | planned |
| Different URLs for the same company and non-Latin title give different keys (job_key.py:147-148; tests/test_job_key.py:93-100) | jobkey.make_key | test_distinct_urls_distinct_keys | planned |
| A title that loses non-Latin letters in the ASCII fold (mixed scripts like "Программист 1С" vs "Аналитик 1С") is lossy: the surviving fragment is only a readable prefix and identity comes from the id/hash (job_key.py:75-95, 125-153; tests/test_job_key.py:114-127) | jobkey.make_key | test_mixed_script_titles_stay_distinct | planned |
| A lossy title with a six-digit URL id keys as fragment-plus-id (e.g. "acme_1-4461771225") (job_key.py:134; tests/test_job_key.py:129-131) | jobkey.make_key | test_lossy_title_prefers_numeric_id | planned |
| Latin letters with no ASCII decomposition (ø, æ, ß, ł) are NOT counted as lossy, so existing keys like "rsted-a-s" and "mrsk" do not re-key; accents and ligatures fold cleanly ("Zürich"->"zurich", "ﬁnance"->"finance") (job_key.py:75-95; tests/test_job_key.py:158-167) | jobkey.lost_letters | test_latin_without_decomposition_not_lossy | planned |
| Non-Latin or mixed-script company names get a hash of the NFC casefolded name (stable across case, normalisation form and URL), so distinct companies never share a key half ("腾讯" vs "阿里巴巴"; "Сбер AI" vs "Яндекс AI"); the Latin fragment stays as a readable prefix (job_key.py:112-124; tests/test_job_key.py:133-143, 179-190) | jobkey.make_key | test_company_fallback_distinct_and_stable | planned |
| Missing or whitespace-only company becomes "unknown-company" (job_key.py:121-122; tests/test_job_key.py:192-195) | jobkey.make_key | test_empty_company_is_unknown_company | planned |
| A canonical key is two non-empty lowercase alnum/hyphen halves around exactly one underscore; keys with "/", ",", parentheses or a bare "company_" are not canonical (job_key.py:156-164; tests/test_job_key.py:198-208) | jobkey.is_canonical | test_canonical_rejects_path_breaking_keys | planned |
| Old three-part keys are recognised as legacy shape, kept apart from malformed keys, and are harmless (job_key.py:167-177; tests/test_job_key.py:210-214) | jobkey.is_legacy_shape | test_legacy_shape_distinct_from_malformed | planned |
| An audit reports entry count, malformed keys, legacy keys, duplicate URLs (trailing slash ignored) and the count of keys drifting from the current rule; exit 1 only for malformed keys or duplicate URLs, never for legacy or drift alone (job_key.py:180-217; tests/test_job_key.py:229-257) | jobkey.audit | test_audit_report_and_exit_codes | planned |
| The audit only reports; it never rewrites keys, because a rewritten key breaks tracker company+role matching (SKILL.md:150; job_key.py:203-204) | jobkey.audit | test_audit_never_mutates_state | planned |
| Audit on a non-object file or a non-object entry gives a clear error and exit 1, not a traceback (job_key.py:183-194; tests/test_job_key.py:269-279) | jobkey.audit | test_audit_bad_shapes_clean_errors | planned |
| A key must be safe as an archive folder name (no path separators) (job_key.py:8-15, 162-164) | jobkey.is_canonical | test_key_safe_as_folder_name | planned |
| CLI prints one key line, requires company and title, and forces UTF-8 output on any host (job_key.py:220-247) | jobkey.cli | test_cli_prints_key_utf8 | planned |

### Rank: fetching and unreachable-vs-expired (rank.md Step 2, rank_state.py apply)

| Original guarantee (file:line) | Where it lives here | How it's checked (test name) | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| A posting is scored only from content actually fetched, never from the title alone and never from invented posting content (rank.md:58, 203) | fit.score / fetch | test_score_refused_without_fetched_text | planned (stricter: score function requires fetched text in its input) |
| Fetch follows the escalation order: installed portal CLI `detail` for that portal's URLs, else plain fetch, then curl with browser headers if robots.txt permits, then a search for the employer's own careers page (rank.md:58, 61) | fetch.escalate | test_escalation_order | planned |
| The browser-header retry is attempted only after a robots check that fails closed (unreadable robots = not permitted) (rank.md:61 says only "if robots.txt permits"; fail-closed is from the rebuild brief) | guards.robots + fetch.escalate | test_header_retry_blocked_when_robots_unreadable | planned (stricter: RFC 9309 parser, fail-closed on 5xx/timeouts) |
| A plain-fetch 403 is a rejected client, not a missing page; the browser-header retry recovers most corporate and bank domains (rank.md:61) | fetch.escalate | test_403_triggers_header_retry_not_closed | planned |
| A stored URL ending in #fragment points at a listing, so the employer's own careers site is searched for the role by name before writing the job off (rank.md:61) | fetch.escalate | test_fragment_url_triggers_careers_page_search | planned |
| `unreachable` and `expired` are never the result of "the first fetch was unhelpful": the full escalation must be exhausted first (rank.md:61) | fetch.escalate | test_unreachable_only_after_full_ladder | planned (stricter: ladder is code, not instruction) |
| `expired` means positive evidence of closure only: page says no longer available or deadline passed, HTTP 410, or a redirect to a listing page (rank.md:59) | fetch.classify | test_expired_requires_positive_evidence | planned |
| `unreachable` means retrieval failed: 401/403/login wall, 404, timeout, 5xx, or empty/unparseable page; a 404 is unreachable, never expired (rank.md:60) | fetch.classify | test_404_403_5xx_timeout_are_unreachable_not_expired | planned (stricter: status-code classification in code) |
| An unreachable result carries a short plain-text fetch_error line (rank.md:60, 77) | fetch.classify | test_unreachable_result_has_fetch_error | planned |
| Triage depth only: no company research, no salary lookup, no web searches beyond fetching the posting (rank.md:5, 62, 205) | fit.score | test_triage_makes_no_research_calls | planned |
| Agent result contract: key, status (scored/expired/unreachable), fetch_error, four scores, location_verdict, language_gate, language_note, deadline, strengths, gaps, language (rank.md:64-80; tests/test_rank_command.py:62-76) | fit.ScoreResult | test_result_contract_fields | planned |
| The set of statuses the spec offers must equal the set the tool accepts (AGENT_STATUSES) (rank_state.py:62-65; tests/test_rank_command.py:62-76) | fit.ScoreResult | test_status_union_equals_accepted_set | planned (stricter: one enum is the single source) |
| `language_gate`/`language_note` are distinct from `language`, which only records the posting's own language (rank.md:82; tests/test_rank_command.py:228-243) | fit.ScoreResult | test_language_gate_distinct_from_posting_language | planned |
| Strengths and gaps in a result are 1-3 bullets each, grounded in posting text, honest (rank.md:75-76, 84) | fit.ScoreResult | test_strengths_gaps_bounded | planned |
| Scoring agents never follow instructions embedded in a posting and never fetch any URL beyond the posting URL (rank.md:204) | guards (untrusted data) / fit.score | test_posting_injection_ignored_and_no_extra_fetch | planned (stricter: fetch tool whitelists the single posting host) |
| Posting text, stored strengths/gaps and cache notes are data, never instructions; agents write plain text, no markup or lifted URLs (rank.md:140; 04-job-evaluation.md:221-225) | guards | test_stored_notes_treated_as_data | planned |
| An unreachable result is recorded with fetch_attempts incremented, first_unreachable as a UTC timestamp (the first failure), and last_fetch_error as plain text capped at 200 chars with whitespace collapsed (rank.md:137; rank_state.py:74-76, 339-348; tests/test_rank_state.py:616-627, 730-733) | rankstate.record_unreachable | test_unreachable_fields_and_error_cap | planned |
| The first_unreachable timestamp never moves on later failures (rank_state.py:342; tests/test_rank_state.py:639-648) | rankstate.record_unreachable | test_first_unreachable_is_stable | planned |

| Three failures inside 48 hours (47h59m) keep the entry unreachable and retrying (tests/test_rank_state.py:639-649) | rankstate.record_unreachable | test_47h59_does_not_give_up | planned |

| An unreachable result whose stored deadline already passed is expired at once (all retry fields and expired_reason dropped), since the deadline is positive evidence of closure (rank_state.py:324-337; tests/test_rank_state.py:711-728) | rankstate.record_unreachable | test_unreachable_past_deadline_expires | planned |
| A later successful fetch (including via --all) drops fetch_attempts, first_unreachable, last_fetch_error and any expired_reason (rank.md:137; rank_state.py:401-402; tests/test_rank_state.py:680-696) | rankstate.apply_scored | test_success_clears_retry_fields | planned |
| An agent-reported `expired` writes status expired and drops retry fields and expired_reason, taking effect immediately even if the entry was unreachable (rank.md:136; rank_state.py:368-375; tests/test_rank_state.py:361-365, 698-709) | rankstate.apply_expired | test_agent_expired_immediate_and_clean | planned |

## C1. The apply workflow and its checklist

# C1: /apply workflow and its checklist (parity matrix slice)

1. Files read completely: apply.md, add-template.md, skills 05/06/08, 04 (cache section + checklist context), CLAUDE.md, cv/main_example.tex, cover_example.tex, cover.cls, and the six named tests (host check, page count, records application, latex guidance, company research cache, add-template build dir). Paths below are relative to the original checkout; `A`=.claude/commands/apply.md, `S`=.claude/skills/job-application-assistant, `T`=tests.
2. Row count: 272 rows.
3. The matrix cannot express: the "spec is the implementation" markdown-wording tests (most of test_apply_records_application.py asserts that sentences exist in .md files; here they become behaviour tests of the loop); the visual Read-the-PDF inspection (kept as a model-judgement step, with code checks as the hard floor); the reviewer's web research quality.
4. Surprises: (a) the host classifier exists ONLY as a reference implementation inside a test (T/test_apply_host_check.py:108-140), the spec is prose, so nothing enforces it at run time; (b) page count, layout holes, ASCII dates, 250-300-word budget, no-em-dash and keyword table are prose-only or tool-only, the loop must make them hard gates; (c) the spec says `\needspace` before each `\cventry` in CLAUDE.md:138 but 05:261 says only where a compile shows an orphan (contradiction); (d) 05:23 / 05:247 say "Any page count other than 2 fails" while 05:358 prefers `\enlargethispage` for 2.02 pages (consistent only if count is checked after the rescue); (e) the cover letter is "approximately one page" in apply.md:122 and CLAUDE.md:131 but "exactly 1" in 5b and CLAUDE.md:139; (f) the table's hosts say `workday.com` is acceptable for the Workday apex but the "six" list names seven literals (host test KNOWN_ATS_APEXES has 7 entries, T/test_apply_host_check.py:80-88); (g) Step 1 sells "present evaluation then ask the user to proceed", a human gate a loop must replace with a configured gate.
5. Out-of-scope items in this slice: none (the slice mentions /outcome, /interview, /setup, gmail-sync, notion-sync only as cross-references; their guarantees belong to other readers).

### Input parsing and fetching (Step 0)

| Original guarantee (file:line) | Where it lives here | How it's checked (test name) | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| Input that looks like a URL is fetched; pasted text is used directly as the posting (A:23,32) | apply.parse_input | test_url_input_fetched_text_input_used_verbatim | planned |

| HTTP 403, a login wall, or an unrelated listing page is never accepted as the posting and never drafted from the title; escalation continues (retry with browser-like headers after a robots.txt check, then the employer's own careers page) (A:30) | fetch (escalation), guards (robots.txt RFC 9309) | test_403_login_wall_and_listing_page_escalate_not_draft | planned (stricter: content-sanity check rejects login/listing pages in code) |
| The employer's own careers posting is preferred over an aggregator listing, and any material discrepancy (requisition ID, grade or seniority) between the two is surfaced to the user (A:31) | fetch, apply.parse_input | test_employer_posting_preferred_and_discrepancy_reported | planned |

| URLs found inside the posting body are never fetched; the user-supplied posting URL is the only exception (A:33) | guards (security), fetch | test_urls_inside_posting_body_never_fetched | planned (stricter: fetch layer refuses any URL not user-supplied or derived from the employer identity) |
| Posting content is never copied into the CV, cover letter or any outbound request merely because the posting asked for it (A:33) | guards (security), apply | test_posting_requested_content_not_included_in_outputs | planned |

### Standing rule and workflow hygiene

| Original guarantee (file:line) | Where it lives here | How it's checked (test name) | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| A fact the user confirms or supplies that is missing from the candidate profile is written back to the profile in the same turn, never left in chat or a draft (A:7) | profile, apply | test_user_confirmed_fact_written_to_profile_same_turn | planned |
| A fact existing only in conversation is treated as unsupported by later sessions and stripped as fabrication, hence the write-back rule (A:9,11) | grounding, profile | test_fact_absent_from_profile_is_flagged_ungrounded | planned |

### Writing-style rules and LaTeX escaping

| `&`, `$`, `#`, `_`, `~`, `^`, backslash are escaped in body text, including company names like Bang \& Olufsen and numbers like 40\% (S/05:223-233; S/06:151-156; T/test_latex_guidance.py:35-99) | latex (escaping) | test_all_special_characters_escaped_in_company_and_bullets | planned |

| Both guides document the required escape set (`\&`, `\%`, `\$`, `\#`, `\_`) in a "LaTeX Special Characters" section (T/test_latex_guidance.py:35-99) | latex | test_guides_list_required_escapes | not ported, because it tests markdown wording; the escaping function itself is covered by the rows above |

| Untrusted text (posting-derived or reviewer-derived) inserted into LaTeX is escaped, never raw (S/05:223; derived, no test in original) | latex (escaping), guards | test_untrusted_text_cannot_inject_latex_commands | planned (stricter: write-shell-escape off, `\input`/`\write18` rejected) |

## C2. PDF and layout verification, salary benchmark

# C2: PDF/layout verification tools and the optional salary benchmark

1. Files read completely: tools/verify_pdf.py, tools/verify_layout.py, salary_lookup.py, tools/convert_salary_excel.py, tools/README_SALARY_TOOL.md, and tests test_verify_pdf, test_verify_layout, test_apply_page_count, test_salary_{chartsheet,company_header,count_only,empty_company,grouping,metadata,nonfinite,unicode}_cli, test_salary_lookup, test_convert_salary_excel, test_convert_salary_excel_integration (all under tests/). Also cross-read CLAUDE.md:142-150 and .claude/commands/apply.md:257-323 because the checks you listed for the text layer live there.
2. Row count: 163 data rows across 9 tables.
3. SURPRISE: verify_pdf.py implements NONE of: `(cid:N)` detection, U+FFFD detection, literal email/phone presence, reading order. Those are prose-only checklist items (CLAUDE.md:144-146, apply.md:313-316) with no code and no test. The tool only does page count, min chars, `--contains`, `--ascii-dates`, `--dump-text`. The loop should implement them in code (stricter). Also: nothing in code ties a keyword-coverage table to the extraction.
4. SURPRISE: the two extractors' missing-tool behaviour differs. verify_pdf FAILS CLOSED (exit 1, install hint) when both pypdf and Poppler are absent, but apply.md:302 says the step degrades gracefully (skip with warning, note in report). verify_layout DEGRADES (exit 2 "skipped:", never a verdict). Also the pypdf path is silently swallowed on any exception and falls to Poppler. test_apply_page_count pins that apply.md text contains `--pages 2` (CV) and `--pages 1` (letter) lines before the layout call; a loop replaces this prose-test with a direct call, so those rows are "stricter".
5. The matrix cannot express: the geometry thresholds are calibrated to one LaTeX template (moderncv/cover.cls, A4); a rebuilt template set must re-derive them (rows say so). The salary tool is mostly Danish-specific and Excel-specific; only the guarantees that survive an arbitrary person-supplied dataset are kept, the rest marked not ported.

Source path prefix: original checkout `src/ai-job-search/`. Tests are in `tests/`.

| If pypdf returns zero characters after normalisation (empty or degraded extraction), it is treated as failure and Poppler is tried (tools/verify_pdf.py:185-188) | pdfcheck.extract_text_layer | test_pypdf_empty_text_falls_back_to_poppler | planned |
| Poppler fallback runs `pdftotext -layout -enc UTF-8 <pdf> -` (the explicit UTF-8 flag is mandatory because xpdf builds default to Latin-1 and would produce replacement characters for a correct non-ASCII CV) (tools/verify_pdf.py:191-192; .claude/commands/apply.md:302) | pdfcheck.extract_text_layer | test_poppler_fallback_command_pins_layout_and_utf8_flags (mirrors tests/test_verify_pdf.py:296-304) | planned |
| The Poppler path also obtains the page count (via `pdfinfo` "Pages:") even when the caller did not ask for a page check, so both extractors return (text, pages) (tools/verify_pdf.py:193-196) | pdfcheck.extract_text_layer | test_fallback_returns_page_count_without_pages_flag | planned |
| A `pdfinfo` output with no "Pages: N" line is an error, never a guessed count (tools/verify_pdf.py:54-58) | pdfcheck.parse_page_count | test_page_count_missing_is_error (tests/test_verify_pdf.py:19-25) | planned |
| The report names which extractor produced the text (`pypdf` or `pdftotext`) in every success message and every failure message, so the report can record it (tools/verify_pdf.py:239,246,252,263,335; .claude/commands/apply.md:296-298) | pdfcheck.verify_pdf | test_result_and_errors_name_the_extractor | planned |

| The CV is checked with `--pages 2` and the cover letter with `--pages 1` in the layout-inspection step; these are the only mechanical page-limit checks (tests/test_apply_page_count.py:407-419; .claude/commands/apply.md:257-263) | apply + pdfcheck.verify_pdf | test_cv_must_be_two_pages_letter_one_page (tests/test_apply_page_count.py:407-419) | planned (stricter: called directly by the loop, no prose to skip) |
| A custom template substitutes its own declared page limit for 2/1 (.claude/commands/apply.md:263) | apply + pdfcheck | test_custom_template_page_limit_overrides_default | planned |
| Page count is checked BEFORE layout geometry runs, because layout deliberately leaves page count to verify_pdf (tests/test_apply_page_count.py:421-426) | apply (ordering) | test_page_count_runs_before_layout | planned |
| No step may defer the page-count check to a step that does not run it (tests/test_apply_page_count.py:428-435) | apply | test_every_deferral_has_a_runnable_check | not ported, because it guards a prose reference between two markdown/Python files; a loop calls the check directly so the drift cannot occur |
| Layout docstring and workflow must agree that page count lives in exactly one place ("two implementations of one rule drift") (tools/verify_layout.py:23-24; tests/test_apply_page_count.py:437-445) | pdfcheck / layout (single owner) | test_page_count_has_one_implementation | planned |

### verify_layout: extraction and degrade behaviour

| Original guarantee (file:line) | Where it lives here | How it's checked (test name) | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| Geometry comes from Poppler word boxes (`pdftotext -bbox -enc UTF-8`); pypdf has no word-bbox equivalent so this step needs Poppler (tools/verify_layout.py:26-28, 186-199) | layout.parse_pdf | test_bbox_parse_builds_pages_and_lines | planned |
| If `pdftotext` is not found, parse raises a skippable error and the CLI exits 2 with `skipped:`; it never invents a layout failure (tools/verify_layout.py:187-188, 411-415; tests/test_verify_layout.py:307-310) | layout.parse_pdf + layout.main | test_missing_poppler_degrades_to_skipped (tests/test_verify_layout.py:307-310) | planned (stricter: skipped is a recorded gate status, and an unverified layout blocks "ready" unless the person waives it) |
| A `pdftotext` that exits non-zero on `-bbox` (xpdf build with no -bbox flag, exit 99; or Poppler 26.0x before 26.05 aborting on an Info dictionary with an empty string, as hyperref writes) is a BROKEN EXTRACTOR, not a broken document: it also degrades to skipped (tools/verify_layout.py:29-35, 200-217; tests/test_verify_layout.py:277-305) | layout.parse_pdf | test_extractor_crash_degrades_to_skipped (tests/test_verify_layout.py:277-283) | planned |
| The skip message names BOTH causes (xpdf shadowing and Poppler empty-Info-string abort/hyperref) plus the first stderr line or the exit code (tools/verify_layout.py:209-217; tests/test_verify_layout.py:285-305) | layout.parse_pdf | test_skip_message_names_both_causes (tests/test_verify_layout.py:285-305) | planned |
| Exit-code contract: 0 clean, 1 layout problem, 2 bad invocation or no usable extractor; exit 2 is NEVER a layout verdict (tools/verify_layout.py:50, 405-424; tests/test_verify_layout.py:312-320; .claude/commands/apply.md:269) | layout.main | test_extractor_failure_exits_2_not_1 (tests/test_verify_layout.py:312-320) | planned |
| A missing PDF file is exit 2 (`error: <pdf> not found`), not 1 (tools/verify_layout.py:407-409) | layout.main | test_missing_file_exits_2 | planned |
| Bbox output is decoded as UTF-8 with replacement so a non-ASCII glyph cannot crash the run (tools/verify_layout.py:190-199) | layout.parse_pdf | test_non_ascii_bbox_output_decodes | planned |
| Words are grouped into lines by ROUNDED yMin (a line = all words sharing round(yMin, 0)); a line's top is the min yMin, bottom the max yMax, left the min xMin, height the max word height, text the words in left-to-right order (tools/verify_layout.py:219-236) | layout.parse_pdf | test_words_grouped_by_rounded_ymin | planned |
| text_left is the xMin of the first word after any leading list-marker words; None when there is no leading marker, or when the line is ONLY markers (tools/verify_layout.py:116-129, 239-244; tests/test_verify_layout.py:252-267) | layout.text_start | test_text_start_sets_aside_leading_markers (tests/test_verify_layout.py:255-267) | planned |
| List markers are Unicode glyphs only: U+2022, U+2023, U+25AA, U+25CF, U+00B7. ASCII `-` and `*`, en/em dashes, and U+25CB are TEXT, because a bullet continuation may begin with a dash (tools/verify_layout.py:88-91; tests/test_verify_layout.py:261-264) | layout.LIST_MARKERS | test_dashes_and_other_glyphs_are_text (tests/test_verify_layout.py:261-264) | planned |
| The layout step is run for both the CV and the cover letter (.claude/commands/apply.md:259-260) | apply + layout | test_layout_runs_for_cv_and_letter | planned |
| The tool prints a per-page measurement line: text y-range, bottom whitespace in pt and %, largest gap and where (tools/verify_layout.py:352-358) | layout.report | test_report_prints_per_page_measurements | planned |
| Compile hygiene: every tools/*.py and tests/*.py must compile with no SyntaxWarning/DeprecationWarning for invalid escape sequences (a docstring quoting a LaTeX macro warned in the middle of a verification report) (tests/test_verify_layout.py:323-360) | repo-level test | test_sources_compile_without_warnings | not ported, because it guards Python 3.10-3.12 escape warnings in docstrings of the original's tools; a rebuilt repo gets this from `python -W error -m compileall` in CI |

### verify_layout: thresholds and what each measures

| Original guarantee (file:line) | Where it lives here | How it's checked (test name) | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| Page count is NOT checked by layout (tools/verify_layout.py:23-24, 344) | layout | test_layout_never_reports_page_count_problems | planned |
| Body = lines whose top is above (page height minus FOOTER_BAND_PT=90pt); everything lower is the footer band and is ignored when measuring the body (tools/verify_layout.py:79-82, 137-140) | layout.Page.body | test_body_excludes_footer_band | planned |
| A page with no body lines is EMPTY and is reported as a problem ("contains no text") in `report` (tools/verify_layout.py:142-144, 347-350; tests/test_verify_layout.py:46-47) | layout.report | test_page_with_no_body_text_is_a_problem (tests/test_verify_layout.py:46-47) | planned |
| Hole: the largest top-to-top distance between consecutive body-line tops (tops rounded to 0.1pt and de-duplicated) must be at most GAP_LIMIT_PT=100pt; larger is a problem naming size, y and ~gap/14 blank lines (tools/verify_layout.py:69-71, 172-183, 360-365) | layout.Page.largest_gap + report | test_hole_over_100pt_is_flagged (tests/test_verify_layout.py:29-52) | planned |
| Gap is measured top-to-top (a tall line inflates it by its own height), deliberately over-detecting; fewer than two distinct tops gives gap 0 (tools/verify_layout.py:172-183) | layout.Page.largest_gap | test_gap_reports_322pt_at_y78 (tests/test_verify_layout.py:37-40) | planned |
| Bottom space = page height minus the bottom of the lowest BODY line (footer ignored), or the whole page height when there is no body (tools/verify_layout.py:146-148) | layout.Page.bottom_space | test_bottom_space_ignores_footer (tests/test_verify_layout.py:42-44) | planned |
| A NON-final page whose bottom space exceeds BOTTOM_LIMIT_FRACTION=0.25 of page height (stock geometry leaves ~85pt) is a problem: it reads as a broken page break (tools/verify_layout.py:73-74, 366-370) | layout.report | test_non_final_page_ending_early_flagged | planned |
| FINAL page emptiness: when there is more than one page and the last page's bottom space exceeds LAST_PAGE_THIN_FRACTION=0.35 of page height, it is a problem ("unfinished document"; restore the highest-relevance content previously cut). A one-page document is exempt (tools/verify_layout.py:76-77, 376-380; shipped example fails by design 52-53) | layout.report | test_last_page_over_35_percent_empty_flagged | planned |
| Footer collision: more than ONE distinct line top (rounded to 0.1pt) inside the bottom 90pt band means body text spilled into the page-number band; exactly one line is the page number and is fine (tools/verify_layout.py:79-82, 159-163, 371-375; tests/test_verify_layout.py:55-61) | layout.Page.footer_crowded | test_two_lines_in_footer_band_flagged (tests/test_verify_layout.py:55-61) | planned |
| The footer-collision advice is to cut content, never to stretch the page with `\enlargethispage` or negative vspace (tools/verify_layout.py:19-21, 371-375) | layout + latex | test_template_has_no_enlargethispage_rescue | planned (stricter: the source is scanned for those macros when a collision or page-count fail occurs) |
| A heading is a line taller than the page's body MEDIAN line height times HEADING_HEIGHT_RATIO=1.25 (line height is a font-size proxy); a page with median 0 has no headings (tools/verify_layout.py:106-107, 154-157, 168-170; tests/test_verify_layout.py:64-77) | layout.Page.is_heading | test_taller_line_is_a_heading (tests/test_verify_layout.py:75-77) | planned |
| A line is indented when its left edge is more than INDENT_PT=8pt past the left edge (page-level or document-level, see orphans) (tools/verify_layout.py:84-86, 165-166; tests/test_verify_layout.py:79-82) | layout.Page.is_indented | test_left_edge_separates_bullets_from_headers | planned |
| Thresholds are calibrated to the stock moderncv CV and cover.cls letter on A4; a registered custom template may need them retuned (an article-class page number outside the 90pt band reads as body text and makes a phantom hole) (tools/verify_layout.py:41-44) | layout (config per template) | test_thresholds_overridable_per_template | planned (stricter: thresholds are per-template config with the footer band derived from the template, not hard-coded) |
| The per-page geometry assumptions are page-height-relative (fractions) except the pt constants (gap 100, band 90, indent 8, set-in 60, same-line 3), so paper size matters; tests use A4 height 842 (tests/test_verify_layout.py:22) | layout | test_letter_size_page_uses_its_own_height | planned |

### verify_layout: orphaned entry headers and stranded headings

| Original guarantee (file:line) | Where it lives here | How it's checked (test name) | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| For each consecutive page pair where both have body text: if the last body line of the page is a section heading, report "ends on the section heading ... content on p<N+1>" and skip further checks for that pair (tools/verify_layout.py:290-302; tests/test_verify_layout.py:85-89) | layout.find_orphans | test_page_ending_on_section_heading_flagged (tests/test_verify_layout.py:85-89) | planned |
| Entry-header orphan: if the next page's first body text starts further in than the text of the page's last printed line by more than INDENT_PT=8pt, the entry was split (header on one page, bullets on the next); report with the shown text truncated to 60 chars and advise `\needspace` or shortening (tools/verify_layout.py:329-338; tests/test_verify_layout.py:91-95) | layout.find_orphans | test_header_orphaned_from_bullets_flagged (tests/test_verify_layout.py:91-95) | planned |
| Indentation for the orphan rule is judged against the DOCUMENT's left margin (min left over all body lines of all pages), not each page's own, so a page that opens with bullets does not take their indent as its margin (tools/verify_layout.py:279-288; tests/test_verify_layout.py:108-112) | layout.find_orphans | test_indent_judged_against_document_margin | planned |
| The page's "last printed line" is every body line whose top is within SAME_PRINTED_LINE_PT=3pt of the last line's top (pdftotext's rounded-yMin buckets split one printed line when a bold word sits a fraction off or a bullet marker sits 1.3pt lower) (tools/verify_layout.py:93-98, 303-304; tests/test_verify_layout.py:202-228) | layout.find_orphans | test_printed_line_split_by_font_read_as_one (tests/test_verify_layout.py:216-223) | planned |
| Where a printed line's text starts is the smallest text_x among its bbox lines that contain at least one word character (`\w`), so marker-only bbox lines are ignored (tools/verify_layout.py:113, 247-250) | layout.printed_text_x | test_text_x_ignores_marker_only_lines | planned |
| Bullet wrapping across the break (marker merged into the first line at x58.9, text at 72.1, continuation at 72.1) is NOT an orphan; read by text start, not line left edge (tools/verify_layout.py:261-268; tests/test_verify_layout.py:132-138) | layout.find_orphans | test_wrapped_bullet_is_not_orphan (tests/test_verify_layout.py:132-138) | planned |
| A wrapped bullet whose text begins with a symbol or an em dash is still a wrapped bullet (tests/test_verify_layout.py:140-152; tools/verify_layout.py:88-91) | layout.find_orphans | test_symbol_or_dash_continuation_is_wrapped_bullet (tests/test_verify_layout.py:140-152) | planned |
| A header level with merged bullets (header at x59.5, next page's bullet line at 58.9 with text at 71.6) IS an orphan (tests/test_verify_layout.py:154-158; tools/verify_layout.py:329-338) | layout.find_orphans | test_header_level_with_merged_bullet_is_orphan (tests/test_verify_layout.py:154-158) | planned |
| Stock-template cases: header text at x70.4 with the outer marker in its own line, and header with marker merged (marker `○` is not in LIST_MARKERS so the line keeps x59.0), and a degree + description pair, all remain reported as orphans (tools/verify_layout.py:270-276; tests/test_verify_layout.py:160-183) | layout.find_orphans | test_stock_header_variants_remain_reported (tests/test_verify_layout.py:160-183) | planned |
| When the marker extracts as its own line and header and description are level, no orphan is reported (the original cannot tell it from a wrapped line without font data) (tools/verify_layout.py:274-276) | layout.find_orphans | test_known_blind_spot_level_marker_line | planned (stricter: with the loop's own template source it can add a LaTeX-side `\needspace`/keep-together check so this case is covered) |
| An outer marker set 1.3pt below a whole one-line item and sorting last is not a split bullet (tests/test_verify_layout.py:185-200; tools/verify_layout.py:303-316) | layout.find_orphans | test_marker_below_whole_item_not_split_bullet (tests/test_verify_layout.py:185-200) | planned |
| A two-line item whose second line opens the next page at the item's text x is read like a wrapped bullet and reported clean (documented behaviour change) (tests/test_verify_layout.py:202-214) | layout.find_orphans | test_two_line_item_split_reads_as_wrapped (tests/test_verify_layout.py:202-214) | planned |
| Lone list marker: when the last printed line has no word characters (only a marker) AND the line is not indented AND the next page's first line is indented, report "bullet split across the page break" with the first 60 chars of the continuation. Different defect, different fix (tools/verify_layout.py:306-316; tests/test_verify_layout.py:97-101) | layout.find_orphans | test_lone_marker_split_bullet_flagged (tests/test_verify_layout.py:97-101) | planned |
| The next page's "opener" is its first body line that has a word character and whose text starts no further than SET_IN_LIMIT_PT=60pt past the document margin; lines set right or centred (running header at x459.8, a lone date line) are skipped (tools/verify_layout.py:100-104, 317-328; tests/test_verify_layout.py:230-249) | layout.find_orphans | test_running_header_is_not_where_body_resumes (tests/test_verify_layout.py:230-240) | planned |
| An orphan hidden behind a running header on the next page is still found (tests/test_verify_layout.py:242-249; tools/verify_layout.py:319-338) | layout.find_orphans | test_orphan_behind_running_header_found (tests/test_verify_layout.py:242-249) | planned |
| If no opener exists on the next page, nothing is reported for that pair; pairs with either page empty are skipped; documents with no body lines return no orphans (tools/verify_layout.py:283-293, 327-328) | layout.find_orphans | test_missing_opener_or_empty_pages_report_nothing | planned |
| A clean break (header and next page level) reports nothing (tests/test_verify_layout.py:103-106; tools/verify_layout.py:331) | layout.find_orphans | test_clean_break_reports_nothing (tests/test_verify_layout.py:103-106) | planned |
| Stock bullet wrapping across the break at the same text x reports nothing (tests/test_verify_layout.py:225-228) | layout.find_orphans | test_stock_bullet_wrap_clean (tests/test_verify_layout.py:225-228) | planned |
| Each reported problem also appears with an actionable fix hint (needspace, shorten, move shorter section above) (tools/verify_layout.py:297-299, 337, 362-365, 373-374, 378-379) | layout.report | test_problems_carry_actionable_hint | planned |
| The tool prints `layout: clean` and returns 0 only when no problem was found; otherwise lists `LAYOUT PROBLEMS:` and returns 1 (tools/verify_layout.py:417-424) | layout.main | test_clean_and_problem_exit_paths | planned |
| In apply the layout failures are fixed by shortening/restructuring content and re-compiling, then re-measuring (loop semantics around the tool; page-limit fixes must not stretch the page) (.claude/commands/apply.md:257-269) | apply (loop) | test_layout_failure_triggers_shorten_and_recompile | planned |

### Salary benchmark (optional; mined for the person-supplied-data version)

The rebuild keeps only an optional benchmark computed from data the person supplies. Proposed module: `salary` (new; not in the planned layout). Input format is whatever the person provides (CSV/JSON); the original's Excel and Danish-header auto-detection is not carried over.

| Original guarantee (file:line) | Where it lives here | How it's checked (test name) | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| The salary step is entirely optional: a missing data file exits with a helpful message and the apply flow skips the step rather than failing (salary_lookup.py:151-161; tools/README_SALARY_TOOL.md:7, 122) | salary.load / apply | test_missing_salary_data_skips_step_without_error | planned |
| The data file is never committed/shared because it may be confidential (tools/README_SALARY_TOOL.md:121) | salary / guards | test_salary_data_is_gitignored_and_not_in_report_by_default | planned (stricter: report/static HTML includes only benchmark results the person opted to show; raw dataset never copied) |
| Data is read as UTF-8 and also tolerates a UTF-8 BOM; a BOM must not hide invalid JSON (the JSON error position is still reported at the right line/column) (salary_lookup.py:163-166; tests/test_salary_lookup.py:630-649) | salary.load | test_bom_and_plain_utf8_both_load / test_bom_does_not_mask_json_error | planned |
| Invalid JSON is reported with line and column and a pointer to the format doc, with exit 1 and no traceback (salary_lookup.py:47-52, 165-166; tests/test_salary_lookup.py:276-293) | salary.load | test_invalid_json_reports_position_without_traceback | planned |
| Validation distinguishes hard errors (exit 1: lookups would crash or emit wrong output) from warnings (exit 0: duplicate company names) (salary_lookup.py:55-62, 139-148; tests/test_salary_lookup.py:332-343) | salary.validate | test_duplicate_company_is_warning_not_error | planned |
| Top level must be an object; `companies` must be a list; `metadata` must be an object when provided (null allowed as absent) (salary_lookup.py:66-87; tests/test_salary_lookup.py:264-274) | salary.validate | test_top_level_shape_errors | planned |
| Each company entry must be an object with a non-empty (non-blank) string name; city must be a string when provided; categories must be an object when provided (salary_lookup.py:90-114; tests/test_salary_lookup.py:295-314) | salary.validate | test_entry_shape_errors | planned |
| Each category value must be an object; count (if present and not null) must be a number; index must be number or string (salary_lookup.py:115-134; tests/test_salary_lookup.py:320-330) | salary.validate | test_category_shape_errors | planned |
| Duplicate company names are detected case-insensitively and reported with both positions (salary_lookup.py:98-106; tests/test_salary_lookup.py:332-343) | salary.validate | test_duplicate_detection_is_case_insensitive | planned |
| metadata.index_baseline must be a FINITE number: text ("150"), bool, null, inf, nan and an int too large for float (10**400) are all rejected by validation with a message naming index_baseline, no traceback (salary_lookup.py:74-80; tests/test_salary_metadata_cli.py:27-49) | salary.validate | test_baseline_rejects_text_bool_null_nonfinite_overflow (tests/test_salary_metadata_cli.py:43-49) | planned |
| metadata.index_label must be a string (list rejected) (salary_lookup.py:81-82; tests/test_salary_metadata_cli.py:38-41) | salary.validate | test_index_label_must_be_string | planned |
| A lookup (not just validate) against a bad baseline also exits 1 with the message and no traceback (salary_lookup.py:144-148; tests/test_salary_metadata_cli.py:32-36) | salary.load | test_lookup_with_bad_baseline_exits_clean | planned |
| Metadata defaults: omitted/empty/null metadata renders with baseline 100 and label "Index" (+65% for index 165); baseline 0 renders no percentage at all; custom baseline 150 gives +10.0% (salary_lookup.py:74,320,338-339,353-358; tests/test_salary_metadata_cli.py:51-60) | salary.render | test_metadata_defaults_and_zero_baseline (tests/test_salary_metadata_cli.py:51-60) | planned |
| Percent vs baseline is ((index - baseline) / baseline) * 100 with one decimal and an explicit + sign for non-negative; zero baseline suppresses the percent (salary_lookup.py:351-358; tests/test_salary_lookup.py:97-125) | salary.render | test_percent_vs_baseline_formula | planned |
| Null metadata or null categories (accepted by validation) must still render, not crash; sibling dict keys act as categories when `categories` is null/empty (salary_lookup.py:316-335; tests/test_salary_lookup.py:127-153, 399-460) | salary.render | test_null_shapes_pass_validate_then_render | planned |
| A count of 0 is shown as 0, never as a missing value (salary_lookup.py:350; tests/test_salary_lookup.py:65-79) | salary.render | test_zero_count_displayed | planned |
| A string index (e.g. "private") renders verbatim without a percent and does not crash (salary_lookup.py:359-361; tests/test_salary_lookup.py:81-95) | salary.render | test_text_index_renders_verbatim | planned |
| Missing vs explicit-null index are different: an absent `index` key shows `-`; an explicit null shows `N/A*` and marks the row as privacy-suppressed (salary_lookup.py:359-368; tests/test_salary_count_only_cli.py:65-71) | salary.render | test_null_index_marked_suppressed_absent_index_dash | planned |
| The privacy footnote ("too few employees to publish") is printed ONLY when at least one row actually rendered N/A*; a count-only dataset must not claim suppression (salary_lookup.py:371-375; tests/test_salary_lookup.py:34-63; tests/test_salary_count_only_cli.py:50-56) | salary.render | test_suppression_footnote_only_when_a_row_is_suppressed | planned |
| Rows with neither count nor index render nothing (salary_lookup.py:349) | salary.render | test_empty_category_row_not_rendered | planned |
| Category label display: underscores to spaces, title-cased (salary_lookup.py:346) | salary.render | test_category_label_display | not ported, because cosmetic and tied to the CLI table; the HTML report formats its own labels |
| Output is UTF-8 whatever the host default; `--json` prints ensure_ascii=False so non-ASCII names round-trip exactly (salary_lookup.py:413-423, 470; tests/test_salary_lookup.py:640-642) | salary / report | test_json_output_round_trips_non_ascii | planned |
| Company-name normalisation: casefold, NFC, then strip legal suffixes and noise (A/S, ApS, I/S, P/S, K/S, IVS, AMBA, dotted A.M.B.A., parentheticals, country/region words, group/holding, and everything after a comma), then keep only alphanumerics and combining marks (salary_lookup.py:36-44, 175-183; tests/test_salary_lookup.py:464-476) | salary.normalize_company | test_normalize_strips_suffixes_and_noise | planned (generalised: suffix list is configurable and ships with common legal forms for the person's market; Danish list kept as default example) |
| Dotted and undotted legal suffixes normalise identically (salary_lookup.py:39; tests/test_salary_lookup.py:170-176, 470-476) | salary.normalize_company | test_dotted_suffix_equals_undotted | planned |
| Non-Latin names must be findable: combining marks and any alphanumeric script survive normalisation, so Chinese/Japanese names do not collapse to empty or to a Latin fragment (`腾讯`, `東京 AI`) (salary_lookup.py:180-183, 204-209; tests/test_salary_unicode_cli.py:42-47) | salary.normalize_company | test_non_latin_names_match_exactly | planned |
| Canonically equivalent spellings match (`Café Étoile` finds `Café Étoile`) (salary_lookup.py:177; tests/test_salary_unicode_cli.py:48-50) | salary.normalize_company | test_nfc_equivalent_spelling_matches | planned |
| ASCII-typed queries find accented names (`Nestle` finds `Nestlé`) and Nordic variants fold (`Orsted`, `Maersk`, `Aaland`) (salary_lookup.py:30-34, 186-194; tests/test_salary_unicode_cli.py:54-62; tests/test_salary_lookup.py:478-481) | salary.fold_accents | test_ascii_query_finds_accented_company | planned |
| Nordic letters fold in a fixed order BEFORE generic accent stripping (ø to o, æ to ae, å to aa, and ö/ä/ü) so `Mærsk`/`Maersk` work (salary_lookup.py:186-194) | salary.fold_accents | test_nordic_fold_precedes_accent_strip | planned |
| Match scoring ladder (0-100): exact normalised equality is 100; substring either way is 80 plus up to 9 from the length ratio; folded equality is 85; folded substring is 75; word overlap with several query words is int(30 + coverage*40); single-word overlap only if the word is present exactly (or folded) giving 70, else 0 (salary_lookup.py:212-272; tests/test_salary_lookup.py:489-506) | salary.match_score | test_score_ladder_values (tests/test_salary_lookup.py:489-506) | planned |
| Short-token guard: a normalised query of 4 chars or fewer that is less than half the entry's length only matches via a shared core word, so `ab` never matches `Something Unrelated Company`; the same guard applies to short entry names inside long queries and to the folded comparison (salary_lookup.py:222-237, 242-250; tests/test_salary_lookup.py:187-195) | salary.match_score | test_short_query_needs_word_overlap | planned |
| Empty query or empty entry name scores 0; unrelated names score 0 (salary_lookup.py:216-217; tests/test_salary_lookup.py:210-219) | salary.match_score | test_empty_and_unrelated_score_zero | planned |

## D. Application lifecycle, interview, upskill, report

# Parity slice D: application lifecycle, interview, upskill, report

1. Files read in full: commands outcome.md, interview.md, html-report.md; skills 07-interview-prep.md and upskill/SKILL.md (the only file in that folder); documents/README.md; tests outcome_followup, outcome_stale, tracker_status_vocab, tracker_notes_csv_safe, upskill_skill, html_report_command. Out-of-scope files (gmail-sync, notion-sync, reset and their tests) were opened only far enough to emit their rows. Also read apply.md Step 6b and setup.md calibration lines, because the vocabulary and "never backwards" rule live there.
2. Row count: 217 table rows (12 topic tables).
3. Not expressible as matrix rows: the conversational turn structure (ask, wait, iterate until the user is happy) is mapped to a confirm/choice gate in the loop, not to a single function; the "hired" ko-fi line and warm congratulation are product copy, kept as one low-priority row.
4. Surprises: (a) "a row never moves backwards" and "only `drafted` is written by the loop" are NOT in outcome.md at all; "never move it backwards" appears only in apply.md:401 and only for re-draft refresh, with no test. Rebuild must make it a code-enforced rank order. (b) Nearly every test in this slice is a substring assertion over markdown prose, so no behavioural test exists for stale math, follow-up counting, funnel math or CSV safety; every row below needs a real behavioural test. (c) test_tracker_status_vocab pins gmail-sync/notion-sync wording, which is out of scope. (d) Stale sweep candidates are limited to `applied` or `interview` (outcome.md:119) while follow-up candidates are any open non-drafted status (outcome.md:92), so `offer` rows are excluded from the sweep but eligible for chasing: an asymmetry, likely intentional.
5. Contradictions: html-report.md:26 says "six canonical buckets" but lists Drafted, Active, Interview, Offer, Hired, Rejected/Closed (six, consistent); the stat-card layout adds a "Sent" card. The calibration step (outcome.md:201-207) says /outcome never writes framework files, whereas the rebuild brief wants calibration to feed the fit framework with confirmation: treat as a deliberate change (planned, stricter).
